### The reasons behind the cells

The reason a can't or not-built cell gives is written for the person, who reads it as `<Provider> can't <action>: <reason>.`, so it names no method, field or version.
The Window shows that sentence in a note under the teammate dialog's field the cell refuses, which stays in place but disabled at its default and names the note as its description: Effort, Speed and Context window; a value saved before is dropped when the teammate is saved.
The composer's Effort, Speed and Plan first rows and the provider menu's Sign out stay in place but disabled, with the sentence as the row's tooltip.
The tooltip shows on hover and when the keyboard reaches the row.
A read-only or unrestricted mode the provider doesn't offer, whose row says it can't or isn't built, shows after the modes as a disabled row named Read-only or Unrestricted, with the sentence as its tooltip in the composer's permissions menu, and as a disabled option in the teammate dialog's mode select, with the sentence in a note under it.
The rewind dialog gives the fork row's sentence, then says the next reply starts a fresh session that is told what was said before; a kind without a table keeps the general sentence.
A running sub-agent's Stop in the Sub-agents view stays in its place but disabled when its reply's provider's Stop one sub-agent row says it can't, with the row's sentence as its tooltip, and stops nothing.
A helper TeamRun runs itself, started through `teamrun_start_sub_agent`, is marked `isTeamRun` and keeps a working Stop under every provider.
Codex registers each sub-agent as a task it can interrupt, but nothing ends what the sub-agent runs in its sandbox.
Sign out's disabled row is left out while the provider signs in or out, as the working row is.
It also shows beside the switch for the provider's own MCP servers; the CLI's `send --effort` refuses with it before anything is sent.
A `/` command Codex, Pi or Claude Code is known to have, other than `/compact` and the four Codex runs through its app-server, sent to Codex or Pi, or to Claude Code where TeamRun would wrap it, answers with its row's sentence, as `<Provider> can't run /<name>: <reason>.`, in a note before anything starts, and keeps the chat's session.
The technical detail behind each one follows, by provider, where the person's text leaves it out.

- Codex, "TeamRun's explorer sub-agent": by Codex's docs, a command a rule allows runs outside the sandbox.
- Codex, "Stop one sub-agent": no call ends what a sub-agent runs in its sandbox, seen with 0.165.0 on Linux (openai/codex#57863).
- GitHub Copilot, "Choose a context size": 1.0.105's `--context` takes only the tiers `default` and `long_context`, for the whole program, and its ACP session names no context size.
  Its documents offer the extended context, about 1M tokens, only for supported models without naming them, and its issues say the tier is ignored under Auto (github/copilot-cli#3481, #3557 and #5324).
  Its /model picker names the size a tier gives, such as "(1.1M context)" (github/copilot-cli#3481), and saves the tier as `contextTier`.
  On Nov 9, 2026 on Linux, turns on `claude-sonnet-5.5`, `claude-opus-5.5`, `gpt-6.1-sol` and `gemini-4-argon` showed the same context size with `long_context` as without it, 200,000 or 272,000, so the test account has no long-context model; building it needs a capture on an account that has one, and a tier to switch, rather than a size, changes the teammate dialog.
- GitHub Copilot, "Rewind within the provider's session": ACP offers no fork capability; its SDK has an experimental `sessions.fork`, outside the decided path.
- GitHub Copilot, "The provider asks the person questions": over ACP, 1.0.105 offers its model no question tool.
- GitHub Copilot, "Tokens and cost of a reply": its prompt answer's `usage` names input, output, cache read and write, and thought tokens, as the draft ACP "End-Turn Token Usage" gives them, seen live with 1.0.105 on Linux on Nov 9, 2026; TeamRun reads them with `AcpUsageReader`, and its context meter from `usage_update`.
  Copilot names no cost, and its input tokens include the cached ones, as TeamRun counts them.
- GitHub Copilot, "Stop one sub-agent": ACP lists none, and its `/tasks` is the terminal's only; its SDK has an experimental `tasks.cancel`, outside the decided path.
- GitHub Copilot, "Follow and stop background commands": over ACP it holds its turn; its SDK has an experimental `shell.kill`, outside the decided path.
- GitHub Copilot, "Generated images in the reply": over ACP, 1.0.105 has no image tool.
- GitHub Copilot, "Shared MCP servers, stdio": 1.0.105 logs a refusal for each one.
- OpenCode, "Choose a fast speed" and "Choose a context size": its source keeps both inside each model, which isn't captured.
- OpenCode, "Rewind within the provider's session": over ACP its fork copies the whole session and takes no message; its server API takes a `messageID`, outside the decided path.
- OpenCode, "The provider asks the person questions": over ACP its bridge forwards no question event; its SDK has `question.asked`, outside the decided path.
- OpenCode, "Plan checklist in the reply": over ACP it sends no plan update; its SDK has to-do events, outside the decided path.
- OpenCode, "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents": over ACP it sends no row events, and a task is a plain tool call; its SDK has child sessions, outside the decided path.
- OpenCode, "A background sub-agent outlives its turn": in OpenCode 1.19.5 its `task` tool takes `background` only with `OPENCODE_EXPERIMENTAL_BACKGROUND_SUBAGENTS`, and a finished background task posts a message that starts the parent's next turn by itself, which its ACP server streams as `session/update` with nothing marking that turn's start or end, so TeamRun could only guess when the wake reply ends, a race it doesn't build; OpenCode's unreleased main line rewrites its ACP server and drops those updates.
  It gets another look once a release ships background sub-agents without the flag and with a turn's start and end over ACP.
- OpenCode and Antigravity, "Stop one sub-agent": ACP gives no sub-agent rows to stop.
- OpenCode, "Generated images in the reply" (not checked): TeamRun reads an image block in a reply, and none has been captured from it.
- Cursor, "Compact the context": `/compact`, `/summarize` and `/compress` sent alone only make the model write a summary, and it lists no compact command.
- Cursor, "Rewind within the provider's session": `session/fork` answers -32601, and its SDK has no fork.
- Cursor, "Choose an effort": over ACP each model is one fixed variant, such as `claude-opus-5-5[context=300k,effort=medium,fast=false]`; `session/set_config_option` and `session/set_model` refuse any other value with -32602 "Invalid model value", and cursor-agent 2026.10.22 lists no model with more than one `effort=` value.
- Cursor, "Choose a fast speed": over ACP each model is one fixed variant whose value carries `fast=true` or `fast=false`, as for "Choose an effort": cursor-agent 2026.10.22's live list has 47 values, one per model, and `session/set_config_option` refuses any value it doesn't list, so the other speed of a model can't be chosen; the test account's plan also refuses `fast=false` for composer-2.5.
- Cursor, "Choose a context size": its model option's values carry one `context=` value per model, `200k`, `256k`, `272k` or `300k`, and on Nov 9, 2026 cursor-agent 2026.10.22's ACP server on Linux answered `session/set_config_option` with -32602 "Invalid params" for `claude-opus-5-5` and `gpt-5.6-sol` with `context=1m`, and for `claude-opus-5-5` with `context=200k`, taking only the listed `context=300k`; its model list named no 1M variant.
- Cursor, "Unrestricted mode": it lists no such mode, and `--yolo` still asks over ACP, so TeamRun's Allow all answers each permission request itself, allowing it once without asking, as Cursor's double shows for a command and an MCP tool.
  No live Cursor turn has run a tool in it yet: that needs an account whose plan lets a prompt run tools, which the free test account's doesn't, and a turn in Allow all that runs a command and an MCP tool with no card.
- Cursor, "The provider asks the person questions": its ACP session has no question tool, and it sends no `cursor/ask_question`.
- Cursor, "Plan checklist in the reply": it sends its to-do list as it works with `cursor/update_todos`, which replaces the list, or with `merge` true updates it by id; TeamRun folds the sent to-dos into the list in order by id, so a later one wins and a new id is added once, and skips a merged to-do without an id, which no later update could match, while a replacing list keeps it; TeamRun keeps each session's list while the app runs, an update between turns too, so the next merge builds on it, and shows it, without cancelled to-dos, as the reply's checklist, as it shows the to-dos of a plan sent with `cursor/create_plan`.
- Cursor, "Plan-limit meters": none in its capture or its SDK types.
- Cursor, "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents": it sends `cursor/task` with the sub-agent's description and prompt.
- Cursor, "Stop one sub-agent": its ACP documents give `cursor/task` only as a notification about a sub-agent's task, which needs no response, and no method to stop one; the client's only stop is `session/cancel`, which cancels the whole prompt turn.
- Cursor, "A background sub-agent outlives its turn": not built yet, and unchecked: its ACP page says `cursor/task` tells the client a sub-agent task completed and how long it ran, and its CLI changelog for 2026.09.15 says a client without the `subagents` capability, as TeamRun is, keeps receiving `cursor/task`, so a background sub-agent's end may come after the turn; when it comes waits on a capture from a plan that lets a prompt start one.
  A client with that capability gets a prompt that waits for its background sub-agents, which holds the turn open, as Copilot's does, rather than outliving it.
- Cursor, "Generated images in the reply": it sends `cursor/generate_image` with the saved file's path, and TeamRun shows that file; checked on its double, which saves a PNG in the chat's folder and names it, as Cursor's ACP documents shape the notification, not in a signed-in session; where and when a signed-in Cursor writes the file is unchecked.
- Cursor, "Switch the person's own servers on": it always reads the person's own `mcp.json`.
- Cursor, "List the person's own servers": TeamRun reads the names in `~/.cursor/mcp.json`, which cursor-agent 2026.10.22's `mcp list` read on Linux on Nov 9, 2026 whatever `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR` said, so every profile lists the same servers.
  `mcp list` connects to each server to show its state, so TeamRun reads the file instead, and a project's `.cursor/mcp.json` isn't listed.
- fx, "Rewind within the provider's session", "Plan-limit meters" and "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents": fx 0.0.16's ACP server has none.
- fx, "Compact the context": fx 0.0.16's ACP server has no `/compact`.
- fx, "TeamRun's approved tools run without asking": fx 0.0.16 names a tool only as `mcp_<server>_<tool>`, which another server's tool can take.
- fx, "The provider asks the person questions": fx 0.0.16 runs an ACP prompt as not interactive (`src/acp/prompt.zig`), so its `ask_user_question` tool has no one to ask, and answers its model "only available in the interactive shell; ask the user freeform instead" (`src/tools/agent/ask_user_question.zig`, `src/core/tooling/tool_runtime.zig`); fx's main after 0.0.16 does the same.
- fx, "Read-only mode": fx 0.0.16's modes in `src/builtins/modes.zig` all keep every tool, with the tool policy `full`.
  Ask asks before a change, but a change the person's fx permission rules allow, or one a "Yes, and don't ask again" answer granted for the session, runs without asking, as fx's permissions page says.
  fx's `read_only` tool policy is used only by a Plan mode in its own tests.
- fx, "Follow and stop background commands": fx 0.0.16's ACP server has no stop for one.
- fx, "Shared MCP servers, stdio" and "Shared MCP servers, HTTP": off until a signed-in capture.
- fx, "List the person's own servers": TeamRun reads the names in `~/.fx/mcp.json`, which fx 0.0.16 reads whatever `FX_HOME` says, as `fx mcp list --json` showed on Linux on Nov 9, 2026.
  A project's `.mcp.json` counts only once approved, and isn't listed, as for Copilot's project files.
- fx, "Several accounts": fx keeps everything in `~/.fx` and reads no `FX_HOME`.
- fx, "Install and update from TeamRun": its Install runs fx's own `curl -fsSL https://fx.sh/setup.sh | bash` in TeamRun's terminal on macOS and Linux, as for a kind TeamRun doesn't install; fx has no Windows build, so on Windows its row shows its page and no Install; updates are the person's own, with `fx upgrade`, as for OpenCode.
- fx, "Show the signed-in account": no account over ACP or in `fx status`.
- fx, "Steer a message into a running turn" (not checked): a message's text goes in and its other files are listed, while one with an image waits for the turn's end; no steered turn has been captured.
- fx, "Plan checklist in the reply": fx 0.0.16's ACP server sends no `plan` update, only message, thought, tool call, command, usage, session info and notice updates (`src/acp/types.zig`), and fx has no to-do tool (`src/tools/`).
- fx, "Generated images in the reply": fx 0.0.16 sends its reply's message and thought chunks and its tool calls' content as text only (`src/acp/types.zig`), and has no tool that makes an image; the only image it sends back is the person's own, when it replays a loaded session.
- fx, "Choose a fast speed": Standard or Fast from its session's `fast` option, set before each turn; fx's turns aren't checked live, so whether a model serves Fast is fx's to say in the option's answer.
  Its Ultra mode, a launch flag, isn't offered, since fx's listing doesn't say which models support it.
- Antigravity, "Rewind within the provider's session": over ACP, 1.3.0 offers only list and resume.
- Antigravity, "Compact the context": `/compact` sent alone answers, and it lists only `/plan` and `/logout`.
- Antigravity, "Choose a fast speed": seen with 1.3.0.
- Antigravity, "Plan mode": its 1.3.0 ACP server has only the Default, Auto Edit and YOLO modes, and lists `/plan`, "Plan carefully before executing a task (generates an implementation plan artifact and awaits user approval)", as a command, not a mode.
  A live `/plan` turn in Default mode on Linux on Nov 9, 2026 asked to run a shell command and to create a file, both refused, then ended with the plan in its reply and no request for its approval; a mode that approves its tools on its own would have run them, and the person could approve the plan only by writing back.
  TeamRun's plan mode needs a mode that keeps a turn from changing anything and a request the person answers to approve the plan, so it isn't built on `/plan`.
- Antigravity, "Plan checklist in the reply": it sends no plan update.
- Antigravity, "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents": over ACP a sub-agent is a plain `start_subagent` tool call whose input names its type and role, seen live with 1.3.0, and its own calls come on the parent's session, so they stay the parent's steps.
- Antigravity, "Shared MCP servers, stdio" and "Shared MCP servers, HTTP": 1.3.0 keeps their values in its session database.
- Antigravity, "Show the signed-in account": its ACP session and its command line don't name the account; only its token file does.
- Pi, "Plan mode" and "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents": its README says it skips them.
- Pi, "Choose a fast speed": no flag, setting or command names a speed, and its OpenAI requests name a service tier only when code passes one.
- Pi, "Choose a context size": each model's context window is fixed; `models.json` can change the number, which only moves where Pi compacts.
- Pi, "The provider asks the person questions": Pi itself asks in its reply's text; an extension's `select`, `confirm`, `input` and `editor` dialogs show as TeamRun's question card, a `confirm`'s title as the card's header over its message, and a dialog's `timeout` closes the card a second early, since Pi sends nothing when it gives up, with a note in the reply that Pi used its default, naming the question, a `confirm` by its title and its message; a dialog that arrives once the turn has ended is dismissed, a `confirm` answered no, without a card.
  The card shows neither an `editor`'s `prefill` nor an `input`'s `placeholder`, since TeamRun's question card has no starting text: the person writes the whole answer, which replaces the prefill.
- Pi, "Plan checklist in the reply" and "Generated images in the reply": its replies carry only text, thinking and tool calls; asked for an image, it drew one into a file with a shell command.
- Pi, "Install and update from TeamRun": its Install runs Pi's own installer in TeamRun's terminal, and `npm i -g @earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` on Windows, as for a kind TeamRun doesn't install; updates are the person's own, with `pi update`, as for OpenCode.
- Pi, "Show the signed-in account": `pi auth check` names only the provider and the kind of sign-in, and RPC has no account command.
- Pi, "Follow and stop background commands": its PR read Pi 1.4.0's source.
- Pi, "TeamRun's tools in a turn": TeamRun's extension adds TeamRun's MCP server over HTTP with `pi.registerMcpServer`, as a `direct` server.
  It reads the address and token from `TEAMRUN_PI_TOOLS_URL` and `TEAMRUN_PI_TOOLS_TOKEN` in the environment of `pi --mode rpc`.
  Every Pi TeamRun starts gets both, empty for a turn without TeamRun's tools, so a Pi never takes the server of a TeamRun whose turn started this one.
  A kept Pi keeps its kept token, which the chat's next turn adopts, and the token is revoked when that Pi closes or fails to start.
  Its tool calls go through the extension's `tool_call` gate like any other tool.
  Pi's v1.1.0 docs give three cases where the server doesn't connect and nothing tells TeamRun: Pi's built-in MCP is off, another extension such as pi-mcp-adapter replaces it, or the person's `mcp.json` has its own server named `teamrun`, which wins.
  TeamRun can't tell these cases apart, but it sees whether the Pi listed TeamRun's tools, which it does once when it connects the server, before its first prompt; a completed turn whose Pi never listed them notes in the chat that TeamRun's tools aren't in it, naming the three cases.
  This rests on Pi's extension and MCP docs at v1.1.0 to v1.5.0, unchecked live.
- Pi, "TeamRun's approved tools run without asking": the extension's `tool_call` gate asks TeamRun with a `confirm`, and `PiTurn` answers it yes without a card when the tool is `mcp__teamrun__<tool>`, Pi's name for an approved TeamRun tool, with each `-` as `_`.
  A Pi in Read only mode still refuses them, and one in Allow all doesn't ask.
  The person's own `mcp.json` server named `teamrun` takes that name, as the note above says, and its tools of an approved name would then run without asking too.
- Pi, "Shared MCP servers, stdio" and "Shared MCP servers, HTTP": TeamRun's extension adds them with `pi.registerMcpServer`, with values from Pi's environment only, as [Per provider](#per-provider) says.
- Muse Code, "Rewind within the provider's session", "Plan-limit meters", "Follow and stop background commands", "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents", "A background sub-agent outlives its turn" and "Stop one sub-agent": `muse acp` offers none.
- Muse Code, "Plan mode", "Read-only mode" and "TeamRun's explorer sub-agent": its ACP guide lists plan mode and read-only mode as missing.
- Muse Code, "Choose a fast speed": `muse acp`'s session offers only the `mode`, `model` and `thought_level` options, as Muse Code 1.4.14's `session/new` answered on Linux x64 on Nov 2, 2026, and no speed.
- Muse Code, "Choose a context size": 1.4.14's `session/new` on Linux on Nov 9, 2026 offered only its `mode`, `model` and `thought_level` options, and no model's value names a context size.
- Muse Code, "Show the signed-in account": its session protocol, as `@muse-code/sdk` 1.4.14 publishes it in `schema/msp/msp.d.ts`, has no account call; the changelog in the same repository ends at 1.4.2, and names the account only on the `/status` card in Muse Code's own window, in an entry under 1.0.1; `muse acp`'s answers to `initialize` and `session/new` haven't been read for one live.
- Muse Code, what each "Not checked" cell rests on:
  - "Run and stop a turn": a live turn reached it, and its account refused the turn.
  - "Resume a chat": through `session/load`.
  - "Compact the context": TeamRun sends `/compact`, which its guide says a fresh install refuses.
  - "Images in a message" and "Files in a message": images are offered, and files go as a path and a resource link.
  - "Permission cards", "The provider asks the person questions" and "Plan checklist in the reply": TeamRun reads approvals, form questions and plan updates.
  - "TeamRun's approved tools run without asking": read-only tools only, if it names them in `_meta["gemini-cli"].mcp` as Gemini CLI does.
  - "TeamRun's tools in a turn" and the shared MCP servers: TeamRun's tools go over HTTP, and the shared servers are passed at a new session.
  - "Sign in from TeamRun", "Sign out from TeamRun" and "Install and update from TeamRun": `muse login`, `muse logout` and its install command, in a terminal.
  - "Several accounts": a profile each, one provider per Mac on macOS.
- Muse Code, each "Not checked" cell: its turns aren't checked live, since there's no paid Muse Code account; the Nov 2, 2026 live run of Muse Code 1.4.14 on Linux x64 checked that TeamRun finds `~/.local/bin/muse` off `PATH`, reads a signed-in profile as signed in and an empty one as signed out, takes the models, approval modes and effort from the session and applies a chosen one, and leaves no `muse` process behind.
- Added ACP agent, "Choose a fast speed", "Choose a context size", "Plan-limit meters", "Rows for the provider's own sub-agents", "Stop one sub-agent" and "Show the signed-in account": ACP names none of them.
- Added ACP agent, "Rewind within the provider's session": ACP's fork is whole-session only.
- Added ACP agent, "A background sub-agent outlives its turn": ACP v1's session updates aren't limited to a prompt turn, but it has no turn an agent starts and no notion of a sub-agent, so TeamRun would have to guess from a late update that a background result came and start the turn for it itself, as it does for Codex; it doesn't guess that, as for OpenCode.
  The proposed v2 prompt, background-work and sub-agent RFDs would add both.
- Added ACP agent, "Several accounts": each kind that has it gives its sign-in a folder of its own through a variable such as `COPILOT_HOME`, but an ACP registry entry names no such variable, and pointing `HOME` elsewhere would change the agent's own tools, such as git and ssh, as fx's section says.
- Added ACP agent, "Read-only mode": ACP's session mode has only an id, a name and a description, and gives no mode id a meaning; refusing each permission request still leaves what the agent runs without asking.
- Added ACP agent, "Plan mode": ACP's session modes have only an id, a name and a description, chosen by the agent, and its config options' categories are `mode`, `model`, `model_config` and `thought_level`, so nothing marks a mode as one that plans (agentclientprotocol.com, "Session Modes" and "Session Config Options"); TeamRun picks no mode by its name.
  ACP's "Exiting plan modes" flow was weighed: a tool the agent offers in its plan mode asks to leave it through a `session/request_permission` the person answers, then a `current_mode_update` names the new mode, but that marks only the way out, so entering the mode would still be a guess by its name.
- Added ACP agent, "Follow and stop background commands": works for a command the agent runs in TeamRun's client terminal, once it outlives its tool call or turn.
  ACP leaves the client terminals optional for an agent, and no live agent has been seen using them yet; an agent that runs its commands itself shows none.
- Added ACP agent, "Tokens and cost of a reply": TeamRun reads the tokens a prompt answer's `usage` names, as the draft ACP "End-Turn Token Usage" gives them, and `usage_update`'s context and cost; an agent that sends no `usage` shows only those.
  The draft says its accounting is still being refined and leaves open whether the cached tokens are part of `inputTokens`: Copilot's are, and its `totalTokens` is input and output alone, while the draft's own example sums the total as input, output, thought and cache write, which puts the cache outside.
  So `AcpUsageReader` counts the cached tokens inside the input only when `totalTokens` is the input and output tokens alone, and otherwise adds them to the input, also when `totalTokens` is missing, as the draft's example has it, so the reply's new input, the input less its cache, is never shown too low.
- Muse Code, "Tokens and cost of a reply": 1.4.14 sends `usage_update` with its context and its cost when known, and its prompt answer names no tokens: its program holds none of the draft's names for them, `cachedReadTokens` and `thoughtTokens`, and no paid account could run a turn to see one.
  TeamRun reads its answer with `AcpUsageReader`, as for any added agent, so a later version that sends `usage` shows its tokens.
- Added ACP agent, "Shared MCP servers, stdio" and "Shared MCP servers, HTTP": off until a capture for that agent.
- Added ACP agent, "List the person's own servers": ACP has no method that lists an agent's own servers, and an added agent names no file it keeps them in.
- Muse Code, "List the person's own servers": TeamRun reads the names in `muse/settings.json` under the `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` Muse Code runs with, the profile's or the person's own.
  Muse Code 1.4.14 has no list command, since `muse mcp` only signs in and out of a server, and on Linux on Nov 9, 2026 it read its servers from that file's `mcpServers` and refused a `schema_version` other than 1; a project's `.muse` settings didn't add any.
  TeamRun refuses the same file with a reason naming it, and reads `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` only when it is an absolute path, as the XDG rules say.
- Pi, "List the person's own servers": TeamRun reads the names in `mcp.json` in Pi's agent folder, the profile's `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` or `~/.pi/agent`, which Pi 1.5.0's `pi mcp list --json` read on Linux on Nov 9, 2026.
  A project's `.pi/mcp.json` counts only in a project the person trusts, and isn't listed.
- Added ACP agent, "Compact the context" and "/compact": works only for an agent that lists `/compact` and reports a completed `compaction_update` for it.
  ACP leaves that report optional, and no live agent has been seen sending it yet; any other answer ends with a note that the context may not be compacted.
- Claude Code, "Steer a message into a running turn": a message added to the running query with priority `next` isn't read by the reply it was sent into; Claude Code answers it after that reply in a native turn of its own, which TeamRun doesn't show.
  Seen in a person's chat: the model answered such a message "No response requested." in a turn TeamRun never stored, and later replies of that session ended with `error_during_execution` and Claude Code's note `[ede_diagnostic] result_type=user`, for a run whose last message is the person's.
- GitHub Copilot, OpenCode, Cursor, Antigravity, Muse Code and an added ACP agent, "Steer a message into a running turn": ACP has no method that adds a message to a running `session/prompt`, and TeamRun knows no extension of theirs that does.
- Codex, "Commands the provider lists" and "Custom commands, prompts and skills": its `/` commands, custom prompts among them, are its terminal app's, and the app-server's `turn/start` takes a message's text as a prompt.
- Codex, "Other built-in commands": TeamRun runs four of its commands through the app-server's own methods instead of `turn/start`; its other commands run only in its terminal app.
  - `/review` runs `review/start` on the chat's thread as the reply's turn: alone it reviews the uncommitted changes, and the text after it is the review's instructions; the reply notes what it reviews and shows the review as its text.
  - `/fork` runs `thread/fork` on the chat's thread, and the chat goes on in the fork, while the earlier thread stays in Codex's history.
  - `/rename <name>` runs `thread/name/set`, which names the thread in Codex's own list; `thread/metadata/update` takes no name.
    `thread/name/set` skips an archived thread and answers that it isn't found, so TeamRun then unarchives the thread and names it again.
  - `/archive` runs `thread/archive`; the chat's next message takes the thread out of the archive, as resuming an archived thread does.
  - The last three start no turn and answer with a note; before the chat's first Codex reply, and `/rename` without a name, they answer with why in a note and send nothing.
- Pi, "Other built-in commands": by Pi's RPC docs, `/settings`, `/model` and the other built-in commands work only in its interactive mode; `/llama` and `/mcp`, which its built-in extensions register and `get_commands` lists, act only there too.
- Antigravity, "Other built-in commands" and "Custom commands, prompts and skills": its `available_commands_update` lists `/plan` and `/logout` only.
- Grok, "Other built-in commands" and "Custom commands, prompts and skills": a signed-in run on Nov 2, 2026 listed 37 commands in `available_commands_update`, among them `/compact`, `/always-approve`, `/context`, `/session-info`, `/review` and `/code-review`, the rest the skills installed in its profile.
- GitHub Copilot, "Other built-in commands": the same signed-in run listed 32, `/add-dir`, `/allow-all`, `/permissions`, `/autopilot`, `/compact`, `/blame`, `/chronicle`, `/context`, `/cwd`, `/env`, `/every`, `/after`, `/fleet`, `/init`, `/list-dirs`, `/mcp`, `/memory`, `/model`, `/plan`, `/plugin`, `/rename`, `/research`, `/remote`, `/reset-allowed-tools`, `/review`, `/sandbox`, `/security-review`, `/session`, `/share`, `/skills`, `/subconscious` and `/usage`.
- GitHub Copilot, "Custom commands, prompts and skills": by its docs a skill runs as a `/` command named for it, but the signed-in run's profile had no skill installed, so whether it lists them in `available_commands_update` waits on a signed-in run with a skill.
- Cursor, "Other built-in commands" and "Custom commands, prompts and skills": its CLI docs name built-in commands such as `/plan`, `/ask`, `/rename`, `/fork` and `/summarize`, and custom commands in `.cursor/commands` and `~/.cursor/commands`, but not which of them its ACP server lists in `available_commands_update`; that waits on a signed-in run.
- fx, "Other built-in commands" and "Custom commands, prompts and skills": its ACP server, at v0.0.16, sends an empty `available_commands_update` after `session/new`, so TeamRun sends no `/` command to it; its skills load through its own skill tool instead.

## Sustain

Sustain keeps a teammate's work going when its account reaches a usage limit, on the next account, model and effort the person listed for it.
The name is TeamRun's own, for this feature inside TeamRun, and is not used as a product name.

### The settings

- A teammate has two more settings, set in its create and edit views: `sustain`, on or off, off for a new teammate and for every teammate stored before it, and `fallbacks`, an ordered list of entries.
  Each entry is an account (which names its provider), a model and an effort; a model or effort of `null` is the provider's default, as on the teammate.
  The teammate's own account, model and effort are always the first entry; `fallbacks` holds only the entries after it, so the list can never lose its first entry or disagree with the teammate's own choice.
- `TeammateCreate` and `TeammateUpdate` take both settings; they refuse an account that doesn't exist, a model the account's provider doesn't list, an entry equal to the first entry or to an earlier one, and an entry on a provider whose limits TeamRun can't read (below), since a limit there would never move the teammate on and every turn would go to it.
  The views list only the providers whose limits TeamRun reads; a new entry's provider list shows the others greyed at its foot, under "Can't read usage limits yet", where none can be chosen.
  A refusal of either setting shows under the list.
  Deleting an account removes its entries from every teammate's list in the same transaction, as it forgets the default teammate's account today.
- Both settings, and the option below as `resetsSoonestFirst`, are kept in the teammate's own JSON like its other settings, so they go out on the change feed with it and are removed with it; `fallbacks` is an array of `{ providerId, model, effort }`, and a teammate stored before them reads as Sustain off, no entries and the option on.
- Each entry after the first shows what a move to it from the entry before it does: "same session" when both are on one provider whose `carriesSessions` is on, "new session with handover" otherwise.
  A move that skips an entry, because that entry has no usage left, goes by the session it leaves, so its note in the chat (below) says which way it went.
- Whether a provider carries sessions between accounts is its own switch, `ProviderKind.carriesSessions`, set per provider from its check below, and on for a provider not yet checked by the assumption the check names, its `sessionsChecked` off, with "same session" shown as "same session, not yet checked" in the views until its check passes; a provider with it off moves between its own accounts by a handover, as between providers, and nothing else in the design depends on which provider it is.
- Whether TeamRun can read a provider's limits is `ProviderKind.reportsLimits`, on for a provider whose limit shape is known (below).
- A teammate whose own account is on a provider with `reportsLimits` off can't turn Sustain on, nor choose a teammate to hand off to when usage runs out (below), since no limit of its would ever start either: `TeammateCreate` and `TeammateUpdate` refuse them, and refuse a change of the teammate's own account to such a provider while either is set.
  The views show both settings unavailable on such a teammate, with "TeamRun can't read <provider>'s usage limits yet"; both become available when that provider's limit capture turns its `reportsLimits` on.
  A teammate moved to such a provider in its edit view has Sustain turned off with the move.
- The list is kept with Sustain off, so turning it on needs nothing else; while it is off, the views show only the switch and its label, and the explanation, the list with Add and the option below appear, with the same entries, once it is on.
- The list has one option, on by default, for a new teammate and for every teammate stored before it: the entry whose usage resets soonest goes first.
  With it on, among the entries after the first that have usage left, the one whose account's soonest plan window resets earliest is taken first, so usage that would reset unused is spent before the others'; the teammate's own entry still goes first while it has usage left.

### What moves a teammate

- Only a usage limit: a provider reports it as its own kind of failure, and the adapter sets `TurnResult.limit`, with the time the limit resets when the provider gives one.
  Every other failure, such as an expired sign-in, an outage or a model that isn't available, shows its error as today and moves nothing.
  - Claude: a `rate_limit_event` whose `rate_limit_info.status` is `rejected` and whose `overageStatus` isn't `allowed` or `allowed_warning`, with `resetsAt` as the reset time, and a turn that ends with an assistant message whose `error` is `rate_limit`.
    A `rate_limit` error without such an event is a passing rate limit, which shows its error and moves nothing.
  - Codex: a turn error whose `codexErrorInfo` is `usageLimitExceeded`; `codex-turn` already reads that field for `contextWindowExceeded`.
  - GitHub Copilot, Cursor, Grok, OpenCode and Antigravity: until a provider's limit is seen in a capture of its own, its `reportsLimits` is off; the checks below didn't reach one.
- A limit is kept in the database, in an `accountLimits` table, a row per account with the time TeamRun saw it and its reset time or `null`, so it outlives a restart.
  A row ends at its reset time; a row with no reset time ends at the first plan usage read that began after the limit was seen and shows room, judged by the read's `readAt`, so a read that began before the limit never ends it.

### Which entry a turn uses

- At the start of each of a teammate's turns, with Sustain on, the engine takes the first entry with usage left, in the list's order or, with the option above, the teammate's own entry and then the entry that resets soonest.
  An entry is out while its account has an `accountLimits` row, or a plan window at 100 % whose `resetsAt` hasn't passed from a read that began after the account's last limit.
  Every entry's provider reports its limits, so an entry that is in either has room or will move the teammate on at its first limit.
- So the teammate returns to its first entry by itself, at the first turn after that entry's usage resets.
- An entry whose sign-in has expired is in like any other; its turns fail with the sign-in error as today, and the person signs in again.
- A turn never moves while it runs: the entry is chosen once, before the turn's request is built, and the reply's provenance records the entry's account, model and effort, as it records the teammate's today.
- When no entry has usage left at a turn's start, the turn runs on the first entry as today, and its limit failure shows as today.
- With Sustain off, nothing here applies: every turn runs on the teammate's own choice.

### The continuation

- A turn that ends on a usage limit keeps what it wrote and ends as failed, its error one line in the error colour that names the account, "Stopped: Claude · Work reached its usage limit", and then its reset, "It resets at 18:00", when the limit or a plan reading names one.
  The account is named "<provider> · <account>", and the reset as an entry's reset below; the error carries `stopped` in its payload, and the window then draws it as a line of its own, from the answer's start edge with no error glyph, unlike other reply errors, and with no used-up line above it, since the line already says when the account resets.
  A plan reading that lands after the failure and shows the plan used up adds its reset to a line that had none.
  A continuation reply then takes the failed reply's place in the chat's replies: it runs next, ahead of any reply already queued, on the next entry with usage left, and answers what the failed reply answered.
  Its prompt and its handover end at the message the failed reply answered, followed by what the failed reply wrote and a line saying it stopped at a usage limit; a message the person sent while the failed turn ran isn't in them, and its own reply, already queued, answers it after the continuation.
- A Stop on the failed reply, or a cancel of the chat's replies, starts no continuation.
- Each entry is tried at most once in one chain: the key is the chain's first reply (a person's message's reply, a wake reply, or the first reply of a send), with the teammate, and the accounts tried are kept in the database in a `sustainAttempts` table, a row per chain, teammate and account with why it was tried, `limit` or `noRoom`, a limit replacing an earlier no room when the account then ran, so the chain tries no account again; the continuation itself lives in memory, so a restart ends the chain as it ends any queued reply.
  A hand-off inside the chain, to a teammate it mentions or to one it hands off to when usage runs out, keeps the chain's key; the rows go with the chat.
- When no entry is left to try, since every entry was tried in the chain, is out of usage or was skipped, the last limit's error shows and nothing more starts, unless the teammate hands its work off when usage runs out (below).
  The last failed reply's stopped line then names why each entry was passed over, in the list's order, in place of its own reset, as "Stopped: Codex · Team reached its usage limit" followed by "Every Sustain entry is out: Claude · Work resets at 18:00, Claude · Personal at 21:30, Codex · Team on Nov 14 at 09:00", each account named "<provider> · <account>", even when the label is the provider's name.
  The first entry still out that names its reset reads "<provider> · <account> resets at 18:00", or "on Nov 14 at 09:00" for a reset after today, in local time, and each later one drops "resets"; one whose limit named no reset reads "<provider> · <account> hasn't said when it resets"; one the chain tried whose limit has ended since reads "<provider> · <account> already stopped at a limit in this reply", and one that can't reply now, such as one turned off, reads "<provider> · <account> can't reply now".
  A reply saved before this shows its earlier note with source `sustain`, "No Sustain entry is left: …", as a muted line with an `info` glyph.
- When the teammate's own account has usage again by then, such as one whose reset passed during the moved turn, the continuation runs on it, unless the chain already tried it.
- A continuation is prepared like any reply of the teammate's: when the teammate's own account can no longer reply, such as one turned off during the failed turn, no continuation starts.
- The window shows each note with source `sustain` as a muted line at the top of its reply, below the header and above its Activity, as it shows a resume note, and keeps it out of the Activity.

### The two ways

- The engine keeps, per chat member, the session and the provider and account it belongs to, and the message the session last saw.
- **The same provider, on another account,** for a provider whose `carriesSessions` is on: the engine makes the session available in the target account's profile and resumes it there, with `resume` as today; the provider's own history goes on.
  How a session becomes available is per provider, from the check: for a provider whose sessions are files in its profile folder, the engine copies them into the target profile under the same id.
  - The copy starts only once the provider has released the session: no query or thread of it is open, and no task of it runs, since Claude keeps its query open while a task runs and Codex keeps its thread for a backgrounded command, and both may still write the file.
    A session not yet released when the move starts goes by a handover instead.
  - Each file is written under a temporary name in the target folder and renamed into place, so a resume never reads a half-written file.
  - The copy is read back, every record parsing and its last record matching the source's, before the resume; a copy that fails either starts a new session with a handover.
  - The source's files stay, so the session can move back; a move back is a copy like any other, and its rename replaces the older copy there with the session as it now is.
- **Another provider:** a new session starts, its first prompt the handover in place of today's fresh prompt.
  The handover is a summary and a selection of what TeamRun holds, in the chat's order, each message with its author and role kept:
  - when the session being left has a compaction, its last compaction summary, read from the provider's files as the check found, and every message of the chat after the point it summarizes;
  - where that summary can't be read, TeamRun's own summary of the chat up to the same point in its place: a request of its own on the target entry, before the turn that moves, through the adapter's `askIsolated`, the same entry as a chat's title: isolated, without TeamRun's tools, and in an ACP agent's restricted mode; its prompt is that part of the chat chosen as the handover's history is, to the target's whole window rather than the handover's 64,000 bytes, since the summary stands for all of it as a provider's own compaction does; its answer is kept per chat and teammate with the sequence number it covers, so a later move from the same compaction, a move back included, reuses it;
  - a target whose adapter runs no isolated turn for its account, such as OpenCode, Copilot with hooks or Cursor on macOS, writes no summary, and the handover takes the chat from its beginning;
  - the move's "Moved to" note shows on the reply before TeamRun's own summary runs, and takes the handover's payload in place once the turn starts, so it says the same whether the summary comes back or the handover falls back to the chat's beginning;
  - TeamRun's own summary waits at most 240 seconds, set from public figures since no account is spent to time one: Artificial Analysis (artificialanalysis.ai, Anthropic's API, its 10,000-token input workload, November 2026) gives Claude Opus 5.5 about 100 output tokens a second and 15.6 s to the first answer token at medium effort, 79 s at extra-high;
    the prompt asks for at most 3,000 words, about 4,000 tokens, which then take 79 s + 4,000 / 100 = 119 s at extra-high, and the 240 s limit doubles that to cover the longer read of a window-sized input, which the benchmark no longer publishes; a summary at maximum effort, 346 s to its first answer token there, runs past it and the handover takes the chat from its beginning;
  - a summary that fails is logged, and the same point isn't asked again until TeamRun restarts;
  - the point a summary covers is the last reply, by the teammate whose session it is, the reply that stopped at the limit included, that holds the provider's "compacted its context" note of its own, which the provider marks with `compaction: true`, not one a sub-agent's run forwards; the summary stands for every message before that reply, the chat's first message apart, and the session's summary is read only when the chat has such a point;
  - when the session has no compaction yet, the chat from its beginning;
  - Claude Code's summary is read from the newest entry marked `isCompactSummary` in `projects/<folder>/<session id>.jsonl` under the session's profile folder, from the file's end back in 1 MiB chunks until that entry, since session files reach hundreds of MB; Codex's is the `message` of the newest `compacted` entry in its `sessions/<year>/<month>/<day>/rollout-<time>-<thread id>.jsonl`, read the same way, but Codex leaves that message empty whenever OpenAI's servers compact the thread, as they do for every ChatGPT or OpenAI sign-in, and keeps the summary encrypted, so those threads take TeamRun's own; OpenCode's sits in its own database, which TeamRun doesn't read while it runs OpenCode over ACP, and OpenCode can't be left by a move until a capture of its limit turns its `reportsLimits` on, so it and the other ACP agents take TeamRun's own for now; Codex and the ACP agents mark their notes, Pi doesn't yet;
  - TeamRun's own summary costs the target account a request, so it runs only for a chat with a point, when the session's summary came back empty, and when no kept summary covers the same point;
  - a read or a summary request that fails is logged, and that handover takes the chat from its beginning;
  - tool calls are written out as one line each with their command or arguments, and a result longer than 2,000 bytes is cut to its start and end, with a line naming its message's sequence number and how much was left out;
  - the message the continuation answers and what the failed reply wrote, partial work included, always whole, apart from the handover's own budget.
- The handover counts one token per UTF-8 byte, including the lines TeamRun adds, which overcounts every model's tokens, since TeamRun counts no tokens itself.
  The target's window is the `windowTokens` of the last context use a reply on that provider kind and model reported, on any account, or 128,000 tokens when none has; a model or account change keeps that window but no earlier usage.
  The prompt leaves room for the provider's own prompt, tools and the work after it, the larger of 16,000 tokens and a quarter of the window, and for the teammate's instructions and role, the handover's opening line, the message it answers, each hand-off it closes with and its attachments, 8,192 tokens for each image and 4,096 for each other file.
  The handover's history gets what is left, at most 64,000 bytes; when the prompt's other texts alone overrun the window, the entry has no room, even with no history to hand over.
  A hand-off quoted whole in its close is left out of the history, its tool calls and results with it, since the close carries its text alone.
- The history is chosen in this order until the budget is spent: the chat's first message, the person's original request; the compaction summary, cut to its start and end if it alone doesn't fit; then turns from the newest back, each whole with its results cut as above, until the next older turn doesn't fit, which leaves it and every older turn out.
- What is left out is named, not lost: the handover ends with a line naming the chat and the sequence numbers left out, and the teammate reads them with `list-messages`, which its instructions already name, as it reads any chat; a cut result names its message the same way.
  If even those lines don't fit, the entry is skipped, which counts as tried, and the continuation goes on to the next one; the message it answers is never shortened to make history fit.
  A continuation checks each entry before it is queued; each attempt of a chain keeps why it was recorded, a usage limit or no room, and the note of a chain with no entry left names a skipped one as "<account> has no room for the chat's handover"; a turn that moves at its start skips in the same way, and when no entry is left it starts with the prompt a new member's session gets.
- In the handover, a tool call is its detail's first line after "Tool: ", a result is the rest of that detail or a provider's tool-result note, and reasoning, errors and TeamRun's own notes are left out; the window is read from the newest reply on the entry's provider kind and model, on any account, that reported its context use.
- Moving to another provider and back is a handover each way: a session left earlier is never resumed after the chat went on in another provider's session, since it lacks those turns.
- No memories are copied or shared, in either way.

### What the chat shows

- A move adds one note to the turn's reply that moves, a continuation's or a new turn's, before its first output: "Moved to <provider>, <account>, <model> because <from account> reached its usage limit; same session" or "…; new session with a handover".
- A move that would have kept its session but couldn't says why after "new session with a handover", as "since the session was still running a task" when the provider hadn't released it.
- The note of a move with a handover opens what the handover carried: the new session's first prompt as it was sent, folded under the note, with its line naming what was left out and how the teammate reads it.
- A return to the first entry adds the same note, with "because its usage reset".
- A move between two accounts of one provider whose `carriesSessions` is on copies the session's file into the other account's profile, at the same relative path, through the adapter's `carrySession`, and resumes the session there, its note ending "; same session", as "Moved to Codex, Team, GPT-5.5 because Personal reached its usage limit; same session".
  Only the session file goes, never a sign-in, and a copy an earlier move left is replaced, so a move back resumes the session as it went on.
  A continuation that keeps its session resumes it with "Your last turn stopped at a usage limit after it wrote this:", what it wrote, and "Go on from where it stopped.", since the session already holds the turn.
  While the provider still runs a background task or sub-agent in the session, nothing is copied and the move hands over, its note ending "…; new session with a handover, since the session was still running a task".
  A session file that isn't found, or a copy that doesn't read back as long as the file, hands over too, with no reason in the note, and a failure is logged.
  Claude and Codex carry sessions; a provider whose adapter has no `carrySession` moves between its own accounts by a handover, as between providers.
  Until Claude's check of a compacted session passes (below), Claude carries only a session with no compaction summary in its file, and a compacted one moves by a handover, with no reason in the note.
- Every other move starts a new session whose first prompt is the handover, its note ending "; new session with a handover", as "Moved to Codex, Team, GPT-5.5 because Personal reached its usage limit; new session with a handover", where the account it moves to is always "<provider>, <account>", even when the label is the provider's name, and the account it left is named by its label alone; a return reads "Moved to Codex, Work, GPT-5.5 because its usage reset".
- The history draws each note as a muted line at the message size, 13 px, after an `arrow_forward` glyph 6 px before its text, at the top of its reply, from the shared `note-line.css` with the message's size and gap on top, with no start-edge bar; the usage hand-off line keeps the label size and gap of `note-line.css`.
- A note whose move started a new session with a handover keeps the prompt it sent in its payload, as `handover`, the text the provider was given, the left-out line naming `list-messages <chatId>` included.
  Beside it, `carried` lists what the handover carried, one line each, from `Handover.carriedOf`: every message whole when they all fit; else the first message by its author, the summary, the later messages kept whole, with tool calls as one line each and the results cut, and those left out; then the message it answers, and for a continuation what the stopped reply wrote.
  The summary's line names the provider kind that wrote it and the messages it covers, `Claude's last compaction summary, covering messages 1 to 41` for the left session's own and `Codex's handover summary, covering messages 1 to 41` for one TeamRun asked for; the messages it covers are not counted as left out, so a handover whose summary covers all it leaves out has no left-out line and no `reads`.
  TeamRun keeps the kind that wrote its own summary beside it (`HandoverSummaryKindsMigration`), so a later move that reuses it names that kind; one kept from before, or of a kind no longer installed, isn't named, and the messages it covers count as left out.
  When it left messages out, `reads` says the target's kind reads them with `list-messages`, as it reads any chat.
  The note ends with a link-coloured "Show handover" button, "Hide handover" once open; it starts folded, and opened, a raised box with a border, 20 px in under the note, is headed "The handover, the new session's first prompt", lists `carried` and ends with `reads`, muted.
  A note without one, such as a return that resumes a session or the note of a chain with no entry left, is the line alone and doesn't open.
  The member keeps the account its session runs on as `sessionProviderId`, so a turn on another account never resumes a session that isn't there.

### When usage runs out: hand off

- A teammate has one more setting among its hand-off settings, in its create and edit views: "Hand off to", an ordered list of other teammates, empty for Off, as it is for a new teammate and for every teammate saved before it.
  It has no name of its own: it is one of TeamRun's hand-offs.
- The teammate's record holds it as `usageHandOffs`, a list of teammate ids, as a shared MCP server holds its per-teammate list, so a teammate saved before it reads an empty list.
  `TeammateCreate` and `TeammateUpdate` refuse the teammate itself, a teammate named twice and one that doesn't exist, and deleting a teammate takes it off every list that names it, recording each teammate it changes.
  They also refuse a list on a teammate whose own provider's limits TeamRun can't read, as they refuse Sustain there, with `usageHandOffs` as the refused field, so the dialog shows the refusal under the Hand off to table, as it shows Sustain's own under its fallback order.
- The dialog draws the list inside Sustain's section, after its settings, as a table like Sustain's fallback order: a saved row names its teammate as text, its provider muted under it, and only a row added or changed in this edit holds the teammate's field; on a teammate whose provider's limits TeamRun can't read, it shows only its heading and Add on one row, each dimmed once, with Add off and described by Sustain's reason half a rem under the row, and saves an empty list.
- It applies only at the start of a turn, when Sustain can't run it: the teammate is out of usage and Sustain is off, or every Sustain entry is out (`SustainRouter.isOutAsync`, above).
  Out means a usage limit; an expired sign-in or an outage shows its error as it does without this setting.
- Then the reply runs no turn and shows one line, "Builder handed off to Reviewer: out of usage".
  A reply of the first teammate on the list that isn't out, still exists and whose provider can start takes its place right after it, answering the same message under its own name, instructions and settings, and that teammate joins the chat if it isn't a member.
  When that teammate already has a reply to the same message in the same send, that reply answers it and no second one is added.
  Its prompt is today's prompt for a reply to that message; Sustain's handover, `ParticipantContext.createHandoverAsync` with that teammate's provider, model and window, takes its place once usage hand-offs use it.
- The reply that ran no turn ends Completed with one note, source `usageHandOff`, whose `handedOff` says whether a teammate took the turn.
  The history draws that reply as the note's line alone, muted at the label size, after an `arrow_forward` glyph when it handed off and an `info` glyph when it didn't, on the reply column's start edge, and the line labels its row; the replies' Activity filter leaves it out.
- A turn that hits a limit while it runs ends as failed, as above, and hands nothing off; the teammate's next turn finds it out at its start and hands off.
  With Sustain on and an entry left, a continuation goes on there; with none left, nothing is queued, and the next turn hands off in the same way.
- A teammate on a provider whose limits TeamRun can't read can be on the list and counts as having usage left; a limit it hits shows its error.
- A teammate still out is never chosen, so a hand-off never goes back to one that ran out, and each usage hand-off counts one hop toward the chain's hand-off limit; at the limit the line reads "Builder is out of usage." followed by the limit's usual note.
- When no teammate on the list has usage left, nothing more starts, and the reply ends with one line that begins "Builder is out of usage, and no teammate it hands off to has usage left."
  The line ends "Nothing more runs until you send again."
- The work doesn't come back: TeamRun keeps no record that it handed off, so after the first teammate's usage resets, its turns start only as they do without this setting.
- The chosen teammate's own Sustain and its own list apply to its turns as to any other.

### The check, per provider

Whether a provider's session can be resumed under another account's profile, and whether its last compaction summary can be read, checked with each provider's own command-line tool on Linux x64 on Nov 1–2, 2026.
Account A starts a session and is told a code word; only the session file is copied into account B's profile folder at the same relative path, never credentials; B resumes the session by its id and is asked for the code word; then A compacts, the file is copied again, and B is asked again.
The maintainer decided on Nov 1 that second accounts exist for Claude and Codex only for now: if both work, the other providers are taken to work too, and each is checked once its second account exists.

- Codex CLI 0.165.0, two ChatGPT accounts, profiles by `CODEX_HOME`:
  - Resume under another account: yes.
    `sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<time>-<thread id>.jsonl` was copied into B's `CODEX_HOME`, and `codex exec resume <thread id>` under B answered the code word on the same thread, again after two automatic compactions under A, so B's account accepts the encrypted compaction item A's account wrote.
  - Compaction summary: not readable.
    A `compacted` line has an empty `message`, and its `replacement_history` holds the person's messages and one `compaction` item with only `encrypted_content`; so a move from Codex to another provider takes TeamRun's own summary in its place, as the handover does when a summary can't be read.
    Codex's source says why, at openai/codex 672e99fed (Nov 12, 2026): `codex-rs/model-provider/src/capabilities.rs:36-39` sets `remote_compaction` to `V2` for the OpenAI provider (and Azure Responses providers), `codex-rs/core/src/tasks/compact.rs:63-70` runs the remote task for `V2` and the local one otherwise, and the remote task writes its `compacted` item with `message: String::new()` (`codex-rs/core/src/compact_remote_v2_installation.rs:246-248`), while only the local task writes the summary text (`codex-rs/core/src/compact.rs:394`, `:446`).
    So TeamRun reads that `message` first and takes it when it isn't empty, which is so only for other model providers, and otherwise writes its own.
  - So Codex's `carriesSessions` is on.
- Claude Code 2.1.312, two claude.ai accounts, profiles by `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`:
  - Resume under another account: yes.
    `projects/<cwd slug>/<session id>.jsonl` was copied into B's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and `claude -p --resume <session id>` under B answered the code word with the same session id, and B's turns went on in its copy (Nov 2, after B's usage reset).
  - Resume after a compaction: not settled.
    On Nov 2, A's usage limit stopped its `/compact`.
    On Nov 12, a partial run: A compacted, writing one `compact_boundary` entry and one `isCompactSummary` entry whose summary names the code word, and B loaded the copy by the same session id and wrote its turn into it, but B's weekly limit ended that turn before an answer.
    It runs again once B's usage resets on Nov 16.
  - Compaction summary: readable as text.
    Compaction writes a `system` entry with subtype `compact_boundary`, then a `user` entry with `isCompactSummary: true` whose content is the summary as a plain string.
  - So Claude carries uncompacted sessions, and a compacted session takes the handover until the compaction case passes.
- OpenCode 1.19.4: the compaction summary is readable as text, an assistant message with `summary: true` whose text part is the summary, readable through the server's `GET /session/:id/message` and stored in `opencode.db` under its data folder; TeamRun runs OpenCode over ACP, with no such server to ask, so it doesn't read that summary yet: OpenCode takes TeamRun's own until a capture of its limit lets a move leave it.
  Its resume with two profile folders waits for its second account.
- GitHub Copilot CLI, Cursor, Grok and Antigravity: each is signed in with one account, and each profile variable was checked: a fresh folder reports it isn't signed in, so a second sign-in lands in its own folder.
  Cursor on Linux needs `CURSOR_DATA_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` in the profile besides `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`, as TeamRun's own sign-in sets them.
  Their resume and compaction-summary checks wait until their second accounts exist; by the assumption above, their `carriesSessions` is on once Claude's and Codex's checks pass, shown as not yet checked, and a provider whose own check then fails turns it off and moves between its own accounts by a handover.

### Specs

- A usage limit moves the teammate to an entry on the same provider, which resumes the same session under the other profile.
- A usage limit moves the teammate to another provider, which starts a new session whose first prompt is the handover.
- A limit with no entry left moves nothing and shows the failure.
- The first entry is used again at the first turn after its reset; a limit kept across a restart still keeps its entry out; a usage read that began before the limit doesn't end it.
- An entry on a provider whose limits TeamRun can't read is refused.
- A limit reported in the middle of a turn ends that turn as failed, and the continuation runs next, ahead of a message the person sent meanwhile, whose reply follows and isn't in the continuation's prompt.
- A Stop on the failed reply, and a cancel of the chat's replies, start no continuation.
- Limits in a row go down the list, each entry once in one chain, a wake reply's chain too, and across a restart; the last limit's error shows when the list runs out.
- A session still held by its provider, or a copy that doesn't read back, moves by a handover; a move back replaces the older copy.
- A provider whose `carriesSessions` is off moves between its own accounts by a handover.
- A move from a session whose summary can't be read hands over TeamRun's own summary and every message since, and a later move from the same compaction reuses it; a session with no compaction hands over the chat from its beginning.
- A handover longer than its budget keeps the first message, the summary and the newest turns, names the sequence numbers it leaves out, never shortens the message it answers, and skips the entry when even the names don't fit.
- An entry's label follows the entry before it.
- Another failure, such as an expired sign-in or a passing Claude rate limit, moves nothing.
- The option is on for a new teammate and a migrated one; with it on, the entry whose usage resets soonest is taken after the teammate's own entry; with it off, the list's order holds.
- With Sustain off, or every entry out, a turn's start finds the teammate out, shows the hand-off line, and the first teammate on its list with usage left takes the reply and keeps the work after the first teammate's reset; with an empty list, the limit's error shows.
- A limit in the middle of a turn with no entry left queues nothing, and the teammate's next turn hands off.
- A teammate still out is never chosen, a usage hand-off counts toward the chain's hand-off limit, and with no teammate on the list left the reply ends with its line.
- The list refuses the teammate itself, a teammate named twice and one that doesn't exist, and deleting a teammate takes it off every list.
- A teammate whose own provider has `reportsLimits` off can't turn Sustain on or choose a teammate to hand off to, nor move its account to such a provider while either is set, and its views say why.
- A provider not yet checked carries sessions between its own accounts, and its entries show "same session, not yet checked".

## Failures

A method that fails answers with a Noldova Desktop failure:

- Its code is the `FailureCode` whose name equals TeamRun's error name, and `Internal` when none does, such as for `ProviderError` and `VersionMismatch`.
- Its message is TeamRun's message.
- Its details are `{ "error": <TeamRun's error name>, "arguments": [<its arguments, in order>] }`, so a client can tell every TeamRun error apart and format its own text.
